<ul class="mw-collapsible-content" style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; line-height: inherit; list-style: none; margin-left: 0;"><li style="line-height: inherit; margin: 0"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><div class="hlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/Sarnami_Hindustani" class="mw-redirect" title="Sarnami Hindustani">Sarnami Hindustani</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Ndyuka_language" title="Ndyuka language">Ndyuka</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Saramaccan_language" title="Saramaccan language">Saramaccan</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Matawai_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Matawai language">Matawai</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Aluku_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Aluku language">Aluku</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Paramaccan_people#Language" title="Paramaccan people">Paramaccan</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Kwinti_language" title="Kwinti language">Kwinti</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Javanese_language" title="Javanese language">Javanese</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language">Chinese</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a></li></ul></div>

<p><b>Suriname</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/ʊər/: &#39;our&#39; in &#39;tour&#39;">ʊər</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span>-<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/c\/c4\/En-us-Suriname.ogg\/En-us-Suriname.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;En-us-Suriname.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/c/c4/En-us-Suriname.ogg/En-us-Suriname.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Suriname.ogg" title="File:En-us-Suriname.ogg">i</a></sup></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">SOOR</span>-ih-<span style="font-size:90%">NAM</span>, -&#8288;<span style="font-size:90%">NAHM</span></i></a>), officially the <b>Republic of Suriname</b> (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Republiek Suriname</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="nl-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Dutch" title="Help:IPA/Dutch">[reːpyˌblik ˌsyːriˈnaːmə]</a></span>, <a href="/wiki/Sranan_Tongo_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Sranan Tongo language">Sranan Tongo</a>: <i lang="srn">Ripoliku Sranan</i><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="I am a Wiktionary editor for Sranan Tongo and I have not been able to document the existence of the word &#39;&#39;ripoliku&#39;&#39; in any digitised corpus of Sranan Tongo texts. Moreover, Sranan Tongo has no constitutional or otherwise official status in Suriname, so it is misleading to include the Sranan Tongo country name in this manner/context. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="srn-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA" title="Help:IPA">[ripoliku sranaŋ]</a></span>), is a country in northern <a href="/wiki/South_America" title="South America">South America</a>. <a href="/wiki/Borders_of_Suriname" title="Borders of Suriname">It is bordered</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">Atlantic Ocean</a> to the north, <a href="/wiki/French_Guiana" title="French Guiana">French Guiana</a> to the east, <a href="/wiki/Guyana" title="Guyana">Guyana</a> to the west, and <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a> to the south. At under 165,000 square kilometers (64,000 square miles), it is <a href="/wiki/List_of_South_American_countries_by_area" title="List of South American countries by area">the smallest country in South America</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It has a population of approximately 612,985,<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> dominated by descendants from the slaves and labourers brought in from <a href="/wiki/Africa" title="Africa">Africa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Asia" title="Asia">Asia</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch Empire">Dutch Empire</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Republic" title="Dutch Republic">Republic</a>. 
Most of the people live by the country's (north) coast, in and around its capital and largest city, <a href="/wiki/Paramaribo" title="Paramaribo">Paramaribo</a>. It is also <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density" title="List of countries and dependencies by population density">one of the least densely populated countries on Earth</a>. Situated slightly north of the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">equator</a>, Suriname is a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical country">tropical country</a> covered in <a href="/wiki/Rainforest" title="Rainforest">rainforests</a>. Its extensive tree cover is vital to the country's efforts to <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_Suriname" title="Climate change in Suriname">mitigate climate change</a> and maintain <a href="/wiki/Carbon_negativity" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon negativity">carbon negativity</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/Developing_country" title="Developing country">developing country</a> with a relatively high <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">level of human development</a>, <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Suriname" title="Economy of Suriname">Suriname's economy</a> is heavily dependent on its abundant natural resources, namely <a href="/wiki/Bauxite" title="Bauxite">bauxite</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gold" title="Gold">gold</a>, <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">petroleum</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Agriculture" title="Agriculture">agricultural products</a>.
</p><p>Suriname was inhabited as early as the fourth millennium BC by various indigenous peoples, including the <a href="/wiki/Arawak" title="Arawak">Arawaks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kalina_people" title="Kalina people">Caribs</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Wayana" title="Wayana">Wayana</a>. Europeans arrived in the 16th century, with the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Republic" title="Dutch Republic">Dutch</a> establishing control over much of the country's current territory by the late 17th century. During the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Surinam" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch Surinam">Dutch colonial period</a>, Suriname was a lucrative source of <a href="/wiki/Sugar" title="Sugar">sugar</a>. Its <a href="/wiki/Plantation_economy" title="Plantation economy">plantation economy</a> was driven by African slave labour, and after the abolition of slavery in 1863, by <a href="/wiki/Indentured_servitude" title="Indentured servitude">indentured servants</a> from Asia, predominantly from <a href="/wiki/British_Raj" title="British Raj">British India</a>, as well as the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_Indies" title="Dutch East Indies">Dutch East Indies</a>. In 1954, Suriname became one of the constituent countries of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>. On 25 November 1975, it left the Kingdom to become an <a href="/wiki/Independence" title="Independence">independent</a> state. In contrast to <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a>'s earlier <a href="/wiki/Indonesian_National_Revolution" title="Indonesian National Revolution">war for independence</a> from the Netherlands, the path toward Suriname's independence was an initiative of the <a href="/wiki/Den_Uyl_cabinet" title="Den Uyl cabinet">then left-wing Dutch government</a>. Suriname continues to maintain close diplomatic, economic, and cultural ties with the Netherlands.
</p><p>Suriname is considered a culturally <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> country, and is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> (CARICOM). It is the only sovereign nation outside of Europe where <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a> is the official and prevailing language of government, business, media, and education.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> According to research by the Dutch Language Union, Dutch is the native language of 60% of Surinamese.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Sranan_Tongo" title="Sranan Tongo">Sranan Tongo</a>, an English-based <a href="/wiki/English_creole" class="mw-redirect" title="English creole">creole language</a>, is a widely used <i><a href="/wiki/Lingua_franca" title="Lingua franca">lingua franca</a></i>. 
</p><p>Suriname is part of several major international and intergovernmental institutions or groupings including the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_Islamic_Cooperation" title="Organisation of Islamic Cooperation">Organisation of Islamic Cooperation</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The name <i>Suriname</i> may derive from an indigenous people called <i>Surinen,</i> who inhabited the area at the time of European contact.<sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The suffix -ame, common in Surinamese river and place names (see also the <a href="/wiki/Coppename_River" title="Coppename River">Coppename River</a>), may come from <i>aima</i> or <i>eima</i>, meaning river or creek mouth, in <a href="/wiki/Lokono_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Lokono language">Lokono</a>, an <a href="/wiki/Arawak_language" title="Arawak language">Arawak language</a> spoken in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The earliest European sources give variants of "Suriname" as the name of the <a href="/wiki/Suriname_River" title="Suriname River">river</a> on which colonies were eventually founded. <a href="/wiki/Lawrence_Kemys" title="Lawrence Kemys">Lawrence Kemys</a> wrote in his <i>Relation of the Second Voyage to Guiana</i> of passing a river called "<i>Shurinama</i>" as he travelled along the coast. In 1598, a fleet of three Dutch ships visiting the Wild Coast mention passing the river "<i>Surinamo</i>". In 1617, a Dutch notary spelled the name of the river on which a Dutch trading post had existed three years earlier as "<i>Surrenant</i>".<sup id="cite_ref-De_Naam_Suriname_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Naam_Suriname-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>British settlers, who in 1630 founded the first European colony at Marshall's Creek along the Suriname River, spelled the name as "<i>Surinam</i>"; this would long remain the standard spelling in English.<sup id="cite_ref-Marshall_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Marshall-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch navigator <a href="/wiki/David_Pietersz._de_Vries" title="David Pietersz. de Vries">David Pietersz. de Vries</a> wrote of travelling up the "<i>Sername</i>" river in 1634 until he encountered the English colony there; the terminal vowel remained in future Dutch spellings and pronunciations. In 1640, a Spanish manuscript entitled "General Description of All His Majesty's Dominions in America" called the river "<i>Soronama</i>". In 1653, instructions given to a British fleet sailing to meet <a href="/wiki/Francis_Willoughby,_5th_Baron_Willoughby_of_Parham" title="Francis Willoughby, 5th Baron Willoughby of Parham">Lord Willoughby</a> in <a href="/wiki/Barbados" title="Barbados">Barbados</a>, which at the time was the seat of English colonial government in the region, again spelled the name of the colony "<i>Surinam</i>". A 1663 royal charter said the region around the river was "called Serrinam also Surrinam".<sup id="cite_ref-De_Naam_Suriname_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Naam_Suriname-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a result of the "<i>Surrinam</i>" spelling, 19th-century British sources offered the folk etymology "<i>Surryham</i>", saying it was the name given to the Suriname River by Lord Willoughby in the 1660s in honour of the <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Howard,_5th_Duke_of_Norfolk" title="Thomas Howard, 5th Duke of Norfolk">Duke of Norfolk and Earl of Surrey</a> when an English <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(English_colony)" title="Surinam (English colony)">colony</a> was established under a grant from King <a href="/wiki/Charles_II_of_England" title="Charles II of England">Charles II</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-De_Naam_Suriname_20-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Naam_Suriname-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> This folk etymology can be found repeated in later English-language sources.<sup id="cite_ref-International_Boundaries:_A_Case_Study_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-International_Boundaries:_A_Case_Study-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-United_Services_Magazine_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-United_Services_Magazine-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When the territory was taken over by the Dutch, it became part of a group of colonies known as <a href="/wiki/Dutch_colonisation_of_the_Guianas" title="Dutch colonisation of the Guianas">Dutch Guiana</a>. The official spelling of the country's English name was changed from "Surinam" to "Suriname" in January 1978, but "Surinam" can still be found in English, such as Suriname's national airline <a href="/wiki/Surinam_Airways" title="Surinam Airways">Surinam Airways</a>. The older English name is reflected in the English pronunciation, <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/sj/: &#39;s&#39; in &#39;consume&#39;">sj</span><span title="/ʊər/: &#39;our&#39; in &#39;tour&#39;">ʊər</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span>-<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span></span>/</a></span></span>. In <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>, the official language of Suriname, the pronunciation is <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="nl-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Dutch" title="Help:IPA/Dutch">[ˌsyriˈnaːmə]</a></span>, with the main stress on the third syllable and a <a href="/wiki/Schwa" title="Schwa">schwa</a> terminal vowel.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Maroon_village,_Suriname_River,_1955.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Maroon_village%2C_Suriname_River%2C_1955.jpg/220px-Maroon_village%2C_Suriname_River%2C_1955.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Maroon_village%2C_Suriname_River%2C_1955.jpg/330px-Maroon_village%2C_Suriname_River%2C_1955.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Maroon_village%2C_Suriname_River%2C_1955.jpg/440px-Maroon_village%2C_Suriname_River%2C_1955.jpg 2x" data-file-width="652" data-file-height="444" /></a><figcaption>Maroon village, along <a href="/wiki/Suriname_River" title="Suriname River">Suriname River</a>, 1955</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Suriname" title="History of Suriname">History of Suriname</a></div>
<p>Indigenous settlement of Suriname dates back to 3,000 BC. The largest tribes were the <a href="/wiki/Arawak" title="Arawak">Arawak</a>, a nomadic coastal tribe that lived from hunting and fishing. They were the first inhabitants in the area. The <a href="/wiki/Kalina_people" title="Kalina people">Carib</a> also settled in the area and conquered the Arawak by using their superior sailing ships. They settled in Galibi (<i>Kupali Yumï,</i> meaning "tree of the forefathers") at the mouth of the <a href="/wiki/Marowijne_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Marowijne River">Marowijne River</a>. While the larger Arawak and Carib tribes lived along the coast and savanna, smaller groups of indigenous people lived in the inland rainforest, such as the <a href="/wiki/Akurio_people" title="Akurio people">Akurio</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tiriy%C3%B3_people" title="Tiriyó people">Trió</a>, <a href="/wiki/Warrau" class="mw-redirect" title="Warrau">Warrau</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Wayana" title="Wayana">Wayana</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Colonial_period">Colonial period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Colonial period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Presidential_palace,_Paramaribo,_Suriname.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Presidential_palace%2C_Paramaribo%2C_Suriname.jpg/220px-Presidential_palace%2C_Paramaribo%2C_Suriname.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Presidential_palace%2C_Paramaribo%2C_Suriname.jpg/330px-Presidential_palace%2C_Paramaribo%2C_Suriname.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Presidential_palace%2C_Paramaribo%2C_Suriname.jpg/440px-Presidential_palace%2C_Paramaribo%2C_Suriname.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="375" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Presidential_Palace_of_Suriname" title="Presidential Palace of Suriname">Presidential Palace of Suriname</a></figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(English_colony)" title="Surinam (English colony)">Surinam (English colony)</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(Dutch_colony)" title="Surinam (Dutch colony)">Surinam (Dutch colony)</a></div>
<p>Beginning in the 16th century, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_France" title="Kingdom of France">French</a>, <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_Spain" title="Habsburg Spain">Spanish</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_England" title="Kingdom of England">English</a> explorers visited the area. A century later, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Republic" title="Dutch Republic">Dutch</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_England" title="Kingdom of England">English</a> settlers established <a href="/wiki/Plantation" title="Plantation">plantation</a> colonies along the many rivers in the fertile Guiana plains. The earliest documented colony in <a href="/wiki/The_Guianas" title="The Guianas">Guiana</a> was an English settlement named Marshall's Creek along the Suriname River.<sup id="cite_ref-Marshall_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Marshall-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> After that there was another short-lived English colony called <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(English_colony)" title="Surinam (English colony)">Surinam</a> that lasted from 1650 to 1667.
</p><p>Disputes arose between the Dutch and the English for control of this territory. In 1667, during negotiations leading to the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Breda_(1667)" title="Treaty of Breda (1667)">Treaty of Breda</a> after the <a href="/wiki/Second_Anglo-Dutch_War" title="Second Anglo-Dutch War">Second Anglo-Dutch War</a>, the Dutch decided to keep the nascent plantation colony of Surinam they had gained from the English. In return the English kept <a href="/wiki/New_Amsterdam" title="New Amsterdam">New Amsterdam</a>, the main city of the former colony of <a href="/wiki/New_Netherland" title="New Netherland">New Netherland</a> in North America on the mid-Atlantic coast. The British renamed it <a href="/wiki/New_York_City" title="New York City">New York City</a>, after the <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_York" title="Duke of York">Duke of York</a> who would later become King <a href="/wiki/James_II_of_England" title="James II of England">James&#160;II of England</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In 1683, the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Suriname" title="Society of Suriname">Society of Suriname</a> was founded by the city of <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam" title="Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Cornelis_van_Aerssen_van_Sommelsdijck" title="Cornelis van Aerssen van Sommelsdijck">Van Aerssen van Sommelsdijck</a> family, and the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_West_India_Company" title="Dutch West India Company">Dutch West India Company</a>. The society was chartered to manage and defend the colony. The planters of the colony relied heavily on <a href="/wiki/Slavery_in_Africa" title="Slavery in Africa">African slaves</a> to cultivate, harvest and process the commodity crops of coffee, cocoa, sugar cane and cotton plantations along the rivers. Planters' treatment of the slaves was notoriously brutal even by the standards of the time<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>—historian <a href="/wiki/C._R._Boxer" title="C. R. Boxer">C. R. Boxer</a> wrote that "man's inhumanity to man just about reached its limits in Surinam"<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>—and many slaves escaped the plantations. In November 1795, the Society was nationalized by the <a href="/wiki/Batavian_Republic" title="Batavian Republic">Batavian Republic</a> and from then on the Batavian Republic and its legal successors (the Kingdom of Holland and the Kingdom of the Netherlands) governed the territory as a national colony, barring a period of British occupation between 1799 and 1802, and between 1804 and 1816.
</p><p>With the help of the <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas" title="Indigenous peoples of the Americas">native South Americans</a> living in the adjoining rain forests, these runaway slaves established a new and unique culture in the interior that was highly successful in its own right. They were known collectively in English as <a href="/wiki/Maroon_(people)" class="mw-redirect" title="Maroon (people)">Maroons</a>, in French as <i>Nèg'Marrons</i> (literally meaning "brown negroes", that is "pale-skinned negroes"), and in Dutch as <i>Marrons.</i>  The Maroons gradually developed several independent tribes through a process of <a href="/wiki/Ethnogenesis" title="Ethnogenesis">ethnogenesis</a>, as they were made up of slaves from different African ethnicities. These tribes include the <a href="/wiki/Saramaka" title="Saramaka">Saramaka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Paramaka" class="mw-redirect" title="Paramaka">Paramaka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ndyuka_people" title="Ndyuka people">Ndyuka</a> or Aukan, <a href="/wiki/Kwinti" class="mw-redirect" title="Kwinti">Kwinti</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aluku" title="Aluku">Aluku</a> or Boni, and <a href="/wiki/Matawai_people" title="Matawai people">Matawai</a>.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Water-front_houses_in_Paramaribo,_1955.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Water-front_houses_in_Paramaribo%2C_1955.jpg/220px-Water-front_houses_in_Paramaribo%2C_1955.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="228" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Water-front_houses_in_Paramaribo%2C_1955.jpg/330px-Water-front_houses_in_Paramaribo%2C_1955.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Water-front_houses_in_Paramaribo%2C_1955.jpg/440px-Water-front_houses_in_Paramaribo%2C_1955.jpg 2x" data-file-width="671" data-file-height="694" /></a><figcaption>Waterfront houses in <a href="/wiki/Paramaribo" title="Paramaribo">Paramaribo</a>, 1955</figcaption></figure>
<p>The Maroons often raided plantations to recruit new members from the slaves and capture women, as well as to acquire weapons, food, and supplies. They sometimes killed planters and their families in the raids. Colonists built defenses, which were significant enough that they were shown on 18th-century maps.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The colonists also mounted armed campaigns against the Maroons, who generally escaped through the rainforest, which they knew much better than the colonists did. To end hostilities, in the 18th century, the European colonial authorities signed several peace treaties with different tribes. They granted the Maroons sovereign status and trade rights in their inland territories, giving them autonomy.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Abolition_of_slavery">Abolition of slavery</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Abolition of slavery">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Suriname" title="Human rights in Suriname">Human rights in Suriname</a></div>
<p>From 1861 to 1863, with the <a href="/wiki/American_Civil_War" title="American Civil War">American Civil War</a> underway, and enslaved people escaping to Northern territory controlled by the <a href="/wiki/Union_(American_Civil_War)" title="Union (American Civil War)">Union</a>, United States President <a href="/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln" title="Abraham Lincoln">Abraham Lincoln</a> and <a href="/wiki/Presidency_of_Abraham_Lincoln" title="Presidency of Abraham Lincoln">his administration</a> looked abroad for places to relocate people who were freed from <a href="/wiki/Enslavement" class="mw-redirect" title="Enslavement">enslavement</a> and who wanted to leave the United States. It opened negotiations with the Dutch government regarding African-American emigration to and colonization of the <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(Dutch_colony)" title="Surinam (Dutch colony)">Dutch colony of Suriname</a>. Nothing came of the idea, and the idea was dropped after 1864.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands abolished slavery in Suriname in 1863, under a gradual process that required slaves to work on plantations for 10 transition years for minimal pay, which was considered as partial compensation for their masters. After that transition period expired in 1873, most <a href="/wiki/Freedmen" class="mw-redirect" title="Freedmen">freedmen</a> largely abandoned the plantations where they had worked for several generations in favor of the capital city, <a href="/wiki/Paramaribo" title="Paramaribo">Paramaribo</a>. Some of them were able to purchase the plantations they worked on, especially in the district of Para and Coronie. Their descendants still live on those grounds today. Several plantation owners did not pay their former enslaved workers the pay they owed them for the ten years following 1863. They paid the workers with the property rights of the ground of the plantation in order to escape their debt to the workers.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Suriname_immigranten_afkomstig_uit_Nederlands-Indi%C3%AB_de_vrouw_rechts_draagt_een_peniti_tak_broche_TMnr_60008927.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Suriname_immigranten_afkomstig_uit_Nederlands-Indi%C3%AB_de_vrouw_rechts_draagt_een_peniti_tak_broche_TMnr_60008927.jpg/220px-COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Suriname_immigranten_afkomstig_uit_Nederlands-Indi%C3%AB_de_vrouw_rechts_draagt_een_peniti_tak_broche_TMnr_60008927.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Suriname_immigranten_afkomstig_uit_Nederlands-Indi%C3%AB_de_vrouw_rechts_draagt_een_peniti_tak_broche_TMnr_60008927.jpg/330px-COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Suriname_immigranten_afkomstig_uit_Nederlands-Indi%C3%AB_de_vrouw_rechts_draagt_een_peniti_tak_broche_TMnr_60008927.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Suriname_immigranten_afkomstig_uit_Nederlands-Indi%C3%AB_de_vrouw_rechts_draagt_een_peniti_tak_broche_TMnr_60008927.jpg/440px-COLLECTIE_TROPENMUSEUM_Suriname_immigranten_afkomstig_uit_Nederlands-Indi%C3%AB_de_vrouw_rechts_draagt_een_peniti_tak_broche_TMnr_60008927.jpg 2x" data-file-width="700" data-file-height="548" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Javanese_people" title="Javanese people">Javanese</a> immigrants brought as contract workers from the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_Indies" title="Dutch East Indies">Dutch East Indies</a>. Picture was taken between 1880 and 1900.</figcaption></figure>
<p>As a plantation colony, Suriname had an economy dependent on labor-intensive commodity crops. To make up for a shortage of labor, the Dutch recruited and transported contract or <a href="/wiki/Indentured_laborer" class="mw-redirect" title="Indentured laborer">indentured laborers</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_Indies" title="Dutch East Indies">Dutch East Indies</a> (modern Indonesia) and India (the latter through an arrangement with the British, who then ruled the area). In addition, during the late 19th and early 20th centuries, small numbers of laborers, mostly men, were recruited from China and the Middle East.
</p><p>Although Suriname's population remains relatively small, because of this complex colonization and exploitation, it is one of the most ethnically and culturally diverse countries in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Decolonization">Decolonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Decolonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Decolonization_of_the_Americas" title="Decolonization of the Americas">Decolonization of the Americas</a> and <a href="/wiki/Suriname_in_World_War_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Suriname in World War II">Suriname in World War II</a></div>
<p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, on 23 November 1941, under an agreement with the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_government-in-exile" title="Dutch government-in-exile">Netherlands government-in-exile</a>, the United States sent 2,000 soldiers to Suriname to protect the <a href="/wiki/Bauxite" title="Bauxite">bauxite</a> mines to support the Allies' war effort.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> In 1942, the Dutch government-in-exile began to review the relations between the Netherlands and its colonies in terms of the post-war period.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1954, Suriname became one of the constituent countries of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>, along with the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a> and the Netherlands. In this construction, the Netherlands retained control of its defense and foreign affairs. In 1974, the local government, led by the <a href="/wiki/National_Party_of_Suriname" title="National Party of Suriname">National Party of Suriname</a> (NPS) (whose membership was largely <a href="/wiki/Creole_peoples" title="Creole peoples">Creole</a>, meaning ethnically African or mixed African-European) started negotiations with the Dutch government leading towards full independence, which was granted on 25 November 1975. A large part of Suriname's economy for the first decade following independence was fueled by foreign aid provided by the Dutch government.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence">Independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Henck_Arron,_Beatrix,_Johan_Ferrier_1975.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Henck_Arron%2C_Beatrix%2C_Johan_Ferrier_1975.jpg/220px-Henck_Arron%2C_Beatrix%2C_Johan_Ferrier_1975.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Henck_Arron%2C_Beatrix%2C_Johan_Ferrier_1975.jpg/330px-Henck_Arron%2C_Beatrix%2C_Johan_Ferrier_1975.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Henck_Arron%2C_Beatrix%2C_Johan_Ferrier_1975.jpg/440px-Henck_Arron%2C_Beatrix%2C_Johan_Ferrier_1975.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2016" data-file-height="2016" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Henck_Arron" title="Henck Arron">Henck Arron</a>, <a href="/wiki/Beatrix_of_the_Netherlands" title="Beatrix of the Netherlands">Beatrix</a> and <a href="/wiki/Johan_Ferrier" title="Johan Ferrier">Johan Ferrier</a> on 25 November 1975</figcaption></figure>
<p>The first President of the country was <a href="/wiki/Johan_Ferrier" title="Johan Ferrier">Johan Ferrier</a>, the former governor, with <a href="/wiki/Henck_Arron" title="Henck Arron">Henck Arron</a> (the then leader of the NPS) as Prime Minister. In the years leading up to independence, nearly one-third of the population of Suriname emigrated to the Netherlands, amidst concern that the new country would fare worse under independence than it had as a constituent country of the Kingdom of the Netherlands. Surinamese politics did degenerate into ethnic polarisation and corruption soon after independence, with the NPS using Dutch aid money for partisan purposes. Its leaders were accused of fraud in the <a href="/wiki/1977_Surinamese_general_election" title="1977 Surinamese general election">1977 elections</a>, in which Arron won a further term, and the discontent was such that a large portion of the population fled to the Netherlands, joining the already significant Surinamese community there.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="1980_military_coup">1980 military coup</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: 1980 military coup">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1980_Surinamese_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="1980 Surinamese coup d&#39;état">1980 Surinamese coup d'état</a></div>
<p>On 25 February 1980, a <a href="/wiki/1980_Surinamese_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="1980 Surinamese coup d&#39;état">military coup</a> overthrew Arron's government. It was initiated by a group of 16 sergeants, led by <a href="/wiki/D%C3%A9si_Bouterse" title="Dési Bouterse">Dési Bouterse</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Opponents of the military regime attempted counter-coups in April 1980, August 1980, 15 March 1981, and again on 12 March 1982. The first counter attempt was led by <a href="/wiki/Fred_Ormskerk" title="Fred Ormskerk">Fred Ormskerk</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Janssen2011_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Janssen2011-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> the second by <a href="/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism" title="Marxism–Leninism">Marxist-Leninists</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> the third by <a href="/wiki/Wilfred_Hawker" title="Wilfred Hawker">Wilfred Hawker</a>, and the fourth by <a href="/wiki/Surendre_Rambocus" title="Surendre Rambocus">Surendre Rambocus</a>.
</p><p>Hawker escaped from prison during the fourth counter-coup attempt, but he was captured and summarily executed. Between 2&#160;am and 5&#160;am on 7 December 1982, the military, under Bouterse's leadership, rounded up 13 prominent citizens who had criticized the military dictatorship and held them at <a href="/wiki/Fort_Zeelandia_(Paramaribo)" title="Fort Zeelandia (Paramaribo)">Fort Zeelandia</a> in Paramaribo.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> The dictatorship had all these men <a href="/wiki/December_murders" title="December murders">executed over the next three days</a>, along with Rambocus and <a href="/wiki/Jiwansingh_Sheombar" class="mw-redirect" title="Jiwansingh Sheombar">Jiwansingh Sheombar</a> (who was also involved in the fourth counter-coup attempt).
</p>
<h3><span id="Civil_war.2C_elections.2C_and_constitution"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Civil_war,_elections,_and_constitution">Civil war, elections, and constitution</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Civil war, elections, and constitution">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The brutal <a href="/wiki/Suriname_Guerrilla_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Suriname Guerrilla War">civil war between the Suriname army and Maroons</a> loyal to rebel leader <a href="/wiki/Ronnie_Brunswijk" title="Ronnie Brunswijk">Ronnie Brunswijk</a>, begun in 1986, continued and its effects further weakened Bouterse's position during the 1990s. Due to the civil war, more than 10,000 Surinamese, mostly Maroons, fled to <a href="/wiki/French_Guiana" title="French Guiana">French Guiana</a> in the late 1980s.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>National elections were held in 1987. The National Assembly adopted a new constitution that allowed Bouterse to remain in charge of the army. Dissatisfied with the government, Bouterse summarily dismissed the ministers in 1990, by telephone. This event became popularly known as the "Telephone Coup". His power began to wane after the 1991 elections.
</p><p>At the <a href="/wiki/1988_Summer_Olympics" title="1988 Summer Olympics">1988 Summer Olympics</a> in <a href="/wiki/Seoul" title="Seoul">Seoul</a>, Suriname became the smallest independent South American state to win its first ever Olympic medal as <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Nesty" title="Anthony Nesty">Anthony Nesty</a> won gold in the <a href="/wiki/Swimming_at_the_1988_Summer_Olympics" title="Swimming at the 1988 Summer Olympics">100-metre butterfly</a>.
</p><p>The first half of 1999 was marked by non-violent <a href="/wiki/1999_Surinamese_protests" title="1999 Surinamese protests">national protests</a> against poor general economic and social conditions.  By mid-year, the Netherlands tried Bouterse <i><a href="/wiki/Trial_in_absentia" title="Trial in absentia">in absentia</a></i> on drug smuggling charges.  He was convicted and sentenced to prison but remained in Suriname.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century">21st century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: 21st century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 19 July 2010, Bouterse returned to power when he was elected as the president of Suriname.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Before his election in 2010, he, along with 24 others, had been charged with the murders of 15 prominent dissidents in the December murders. However, in 2012, two months before the verdict in the trial, the National Assembly extended its <a href="/wiki/Amnesty" title="Amnesty">amnesty</a> law and provided Bouterse and the others with amnesty of these charges. He was reelected on 14 July 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> However, Bouterse was convicted by a Surinamese court on 29 November 2019 and given a 20-year sentence for his role in the 1982 killings.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After winning the <a href="/wiki/2020_Surinamese_general_election" title="2020 Surinamese general election">2020 elections</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Chan_Santokhi" title="Chan Santokhi">Chan Santokhi</a> was the sole nomination for <a href="/wiki/President_of_Suriname" title="President of Suriname">president of Suriname</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> On 13 July, Santokhi was elected president by acclamation in an <a href="/wiki/Uncontested_election" title="Uncontested election">uncontested election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> He was inaugurated on 16 July in a ceremony without public attendance due to the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Suriname" title="COVID-19 pandemic in Suriname">COVID-19 pandemic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 2023, there were heavy protests against rising living costs in the capital Paramaribo. Protesters accused the government of President Chan Santokhi of corruption. They stormed the National Assembly, demanding the government to resign. However, the government condemned the protests.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:AssembleeSurinaam.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/AssembleeSurinaam.jpg/220px-AssembleeSurinaam.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="168" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/AssembleeSurinaam.jpg/330px-AssembleeSurinaam.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/AssembleeSurinaam.jpg/440px-AssembleeSurinaam.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2499" data-file-height="1905" /></a><figcaption>National Assembly</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paramaribo,_Hof_van_Justitie_(Court_of_Justice).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Paramaribo%2C_Hof_van_Justitie_%28Court_of_Justice%29.jpg/220px-Paramaribo%2C_Hof_van_Justitie_%28Court_of_Justice%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Paramaribo%2C_Hof_van_Justitie_%28Court_of_Justice%29.jpg/330px-Paramaribo%2C_Hof_van_Justitie_%28Court_of_Justice%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Paramaribo%2C_Hof_van_Justitie_%28Court_of_Justice%29.jpg/440px-Paramaribo%2C_Hof_van_Justitie_%28Court_of_Justice%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2448" /></a><figcaption>Court of Justice</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Suriname" title="Politics of Suriname">Politics of Suriname</a></div>
<p>The Republic of Suriname is a <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> republic, based on the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Suriname" title="Constitution of Suriname">Constitution of 1987</a>. The legislative branch of government consists of a 51-member <a href="/wiki/Unicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Unicameral">unicameral</a> <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Suriname" class="mw-redirect" title="National Assembly of Suriname">National Assembly</a>, simultaneously and popularly elected for a five-year term.
</p><p>In the elections held on Tuesday, 25 May 2010, the <i>Megacombinatie</i> won 23 of the National Assembly seats followed by <i>Nationale Front</i> with 20 seats. A much smaller number, important for coalition-building, went to the "A-combinatie" and to the <i>Volksalliantie.</i> The parties held negotiations to form coalitions. Elections were held on 25 May 2015, and the National Assembly again elected Dési Bouterse as president.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Suriname" title="President of Suriname">president of Suriname</a> is elected for a five-year term by a two-thirds majority of the National Assembly. If at least two-thirds of the National Assembly cannot agree to vote for one presidential candidate, a People's Assembly is formed from all National Assembly delegates and regional and municipal representatives who were elected by popular vote in the most recent national election. The president may be elected by a majority of the People's Assembly called for the special election.
</p><p>As head of government, the president appoints a sixteen-minister cabinet. A vice president is normally elected for a five-year term at the same time as the president, by a simple majority in the National Assembly or People's Assembly. There is no constitutional provision for removal or replacement of the president, except in the case of resignation.
</p><p>The judiciary is headed by the <a href="/wiki/High_Court_of_Justice_of_Suriname" title="High Court of Justice of Suriname">High Court of Justice of Suriname</a> (Supreme Court). This court supervises the magistrate courts. Members are appointed for life by the president in consultation with the National Assembly, the State Advisory Council, and the National Order of Private Attorneys.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Suriname" title="Foreign relations of Suriname">Foreign relations of Suriname</a></div>
<p>Due to Suriname's Dutch colonial history, Suriname had a long-standing special relationship with the Netherlands.
</p><p>In 1999, Dési Bouterse was convicted and sentenced in absentia in the Netherlands to 11 years of imprisonment for drug trafficking. He was the main suspect in the court case concerning the December murders, the 1982 assassination of opponents of military rule in <a href="/wiki/Fort_Zeelandia_(Paramaribo)" title="Fort Zeelandia (Paramaribo)">Fort Zeelandia</a>, Paramaribo. He served as president between 2010 and 2020. These two cases still strain relations between the Netherlands and Suriname.<sup id="cite_ref-minbuza_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-minbuza-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Dutch government stated during that time that it would maintain limited contact with the president.<sup id="cite_ref-minbuza_52-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-minbuza-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Bouterse was elected as president of Suriname in 2010. The Netherlands in July 2014 dropped Suriname as a member of its development program.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 1991, the United States has maintained positive relations with Suriname. The two countries work together through the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Caribbean_Basin_Security_Initiative&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Caribbean Basin Security Initiative (page does not exist)">Caribbean Basin Security Initiative</a> (CBSI) and the U.S. President's Emergency Plan for AIDS Relief (PEPFAR). Suriname also receives military funding from the U.S. Department of Defense.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> relations and cooperation with Suriname are carried out both on a bilateral and a regional basis. There are ongoing EU-<a href="/wiki/Community_of_Latin_American_and_Caribbean_States" title="Community of Latin American and Caribbean States">Community of Latin American and Caribbean States</a> (CELAC) and EU-<a href="/wiki/CARIFORUM" title="CARIFORUM">CARIFORUM</a> dialogues. Suriname is party to the <a href="/wiki/Cotonou_Agreement" title="Cotonou Agreement">Cotonou Agreement</a>, the partnership agreement among the members of the African, Caribbean and Pacific Group of States and the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 17 February 2005, the leaders of <a href="/wiki/Barbados" title="Barbados">Barbados</a> and Suriname signed the "Agreement for the deepening of bilateral cooperation between the Government of Barbados and the Government of the Republic of Suriname."<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> On 23–24 April 2009, both nations formed a Joint Commission in <a href="/wiki/Paramaribo" title="Paramaribo">Paramaribo</a>, Suriname, to improve relations and to expand into various areas of cooperation.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> They held a second meeting toward this goal on 3–4 March 2011, in Dover, Barbados. Their representatives reviewed issues of agriculture, trade, investment, as well as international transport.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the late 2000s, Suriname intensified development cooperation with other developing countries. China's South-South cooperation with Suriname has included a number of large-scale infrastructure projects, including port rehabilitation and road construction. Brazil signed agreements to cooperate with Suriname in education, health, agriculture, and energy production.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Suriname" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Suriname">Military of Suriname</a></div>
<p>The Armed Forces of Suriname have three branches: the Army, the Air Force, and the Navy. The president of the Republic is the Supreme Commander-in-Chief of the Armed Forces (<i>Opperbevelhebber van de Strijdkrachten</i>). The president is assisted by the minister of defence. Beneath the president and minister of defence is the commander of the armed forces (<i>Bevelhebber van de Strijdkrachten</i>). The military branches and regional military commands report to the commander.
</p><p>After the creation of the <a href="/wiki/Statute_of_the_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Statute of the Kingdom of the Netherlands">Statute of the Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Army" title="Royal Netherlands Army">Royal Netherlands Army</a> was entrusted with the defense of Suriname, while the defense of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a> was the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Navy" title="Royal Netherlands Navy">Royal Netherlands Navy</a>. The army set up a separate <i>Troepenmacht in Suriname</i> (Forces in Suriname, TRIS). Upon independence in 1975, this force was turned into the <i>Surinaamse Krijgsmacht</i> (SKM):, Surinamese Armed Forces. After the 1980 overthrow of the government, the SKM was rebranded as the <i>Nationaal Leger</i> (NL), National Army.
</p><p>In 1965, the Dutch and Americans used Suriname's Coronie site for multiple Nike Apache <a href="/wiki/Sounding_rocket" title="Sounding rocket">sounding rocket</a> launches.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Suriname,_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_-_colored.svg" class="mw-file-description" title="Districts of Suriname"><img alt="Districts of Suriname" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Suriname%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_-_colored.svg/225px-Suriname%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_-_colored.svg.png" decoding="async" width="225" height="230" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Suriname%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_-_colored.svg/338px-Suriname%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_-_colored.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Suriname%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_-_colored.svg/450px-Suriname%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_-_colored.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1181" data-file-height="1205" /></a><figcaption>Districts of Suriname</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Suriname_with_disputed_territories.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Suriname_with_disputed_territories.jpg/220px-Suriname_with_disputed_territories.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="265" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Suriname_with_disputed_territories.jpg/330px-Suriname_with_disputed_territories.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Suriname_with_disputed_territories.jpg/440px-Suriname_with_disputed_territories.jpg 2x" data-file-width="497" data-file-height="599" /></a><figcaption>Map of Suriname with disputed territories in light green.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Suriname_geology_NL_annot.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Suriname_geology_NL_annot.svg/220px-Suriname_geology_NL_annot.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="308" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Suriname_geology_NL_annot.svg/330px-Suriname_geology_NL_annot.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Suriname_geology_NL_annot.svg/440px-Suriname_geology_NL_annot.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="716" data-file-height="1002" /></a><figcaption>Geology of Suriname</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Suriname" title="Districts of Suriname">Districts of Suriname</a> and <a href="/wiki/Resorts_of_Suriname" title="Resorts of Suriname">Resorts of Suriname</a></div>
<p>The country is divided into ten administrative districts, each headed by a district commissioner appointed by the president, who also has the power of dismissal. Suriname is further subdivided into 62 resorts (ressorten).
</p>
