<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Suriname" title="Health in Suriname">Health in Suriname</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Global_Burden_of_Disease" class="mw-redirect" title="Global Burden of Disease">Global Burden of Disease</a> Study provides an on-line data source for analyzing updated estimates of health for 359 diseases and injuries and 84 risk factors from 1990 to 2017 in most of the world's countries.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Comparing Suriname with other Caribbean nations show that in 2017 the age-standardized <a href="/wiki/Death_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Death rate">death rate</a> for all causes was 793 (males 969, females 641) per 100,000, far below the 1219 of <a href="/wiki/Haiti" title="Haiti">Haiti</a>, somewhat below the 944 of <a href="/wiki/Guyana" title="Guyana">Guyana</a> but considerably above the 424 of  <a href="/wiki/Bermuda" title="Bermuda">Bermuda</a>. In 1990, the death rate was 960 per 100,000. <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">Life expectancy</a> in 2017 was 72 years (males 69, females 75). The death rate for children &lt; 5 years was 581 per 100,000 compared to 1308 in Haiti and 102 in Bermuda. In 1990 and 2017, the leading causes of age-standardized death rates were cardiovascular disease, cancer, and diabetes/chronic kidney disease.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Suriname" title="Education in Suriname">Education in Suriname</a></div>
<p>Education in Suriname is compulsory until the age of 12,<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> and the nation had a net primary enrollment rate of 94% in 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Literacy" title="Literacy">Literacy</a> is very common, particularly among men.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_120-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> The main university in the country is the <a href="/wiki/Anton_de_Kom_University_of_Suriname" title="Anton de Kom University of Suriname">Anton de Kom University of Suriname</a>.
</p><p>From elementary school to high school there are 13 grades. The elementary school has six grades, middle school four grades, and high school three grades. Students take a test at the end of elementary school to determine whether they will go to the MULO (secondary modern school) or a middle school of lower standards like LBO.
</p><p>Students going from the second grade of middle school to the third grade have to choose between the business or science courses. This will determine what their major subjects will be. In order to go on to study math and physics, the student must have a total of 12 points. If the student has fewer points, he/she will go into the business courses or fail the grade.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Traditionally, <i><a href="/wiki/De_Ware_Tijd" title="De Ware Tijd">De Ware Tijd</a></i> was the major newspaper of the country, but since the '90s <i><a href="/wiki/Times_of_Suriname" title="Times of Suriname">Times of Suriname</a>, <a href="/wiki/De_West" title="De West">De West</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Dagblad_Suriname" title="Dagblad Suriname">Dagblad Suriname</a></i> have also been well-read newspapers. All publish primarily in Dutch.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Suriname has twenty-four radio stations, most of them also broadcast through the Internet. There are twelve television sources:
<a href="/wiki/Ampie%27s_Broadcasting_Corporation" title="Ampie&#39;s Broadcasting Corporation">ABC</a> (Ch. 4–1, 2), RBN (Ch. 5–1, 2), Rasonic TV (Ch. 7), STVS (Ch. 8–1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6), Apintie (Ch. 10–1), ATV (Ch. 12–1, 2, 3, 4), Radika (Ch. 14), SCCN (Ch. 17–1, 2, 3), Pipel TV (Ch. 18–1, 2), Trishul (Ch. 20–1, 2, 3, 4), Garuda (Ch. 23–1, 2, 3), Sangeetmala (Ch. 26), Ch. 30, Ch. 31, Ch.32, Ch.38, SCTV (Ch. 45). Also listened to is <a href="/wiki/Mart_(broadcaster)" title="Mart (broadcaster)">mArt</a>, a broadcaster from Amsterdam founded by people from Suriname. <a href="/w/index.php?title=Kondreman&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Kondreman (page does not exist)">Kondreman</a> is one of the popular cartoons in Suriname.
</p><p>There are also three major news sites: Starnieuws, Suriname Herald, and GFC Nieuws.
</p><p>In 2022, Suriname ranked 52nd in the worldwide <a href="/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index" class="mw-redirect" title="Press Freedom Index">Press Freedom Index</a> by the organization <a href="/wiki/Reporters_Without_Borders" title="Reporters Without Borders">Reporters Without Borders</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> a strong drop in the ranking compared to the 2018-2021 period (about location 20).
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Amazon_jungle_from_above.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Amazon_jungle_from_above.jpg/220px-Amazon_jungle_from_above.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Amazon_jungle_from_above.jpg/330px-Amazon_jungle_from_above.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Amazon_jungle_from_above.jpg/440px-Amazon_jungle_from_above.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1071" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Central_Suriname_Nature_Reserve" title="Central Suriname Nature Reserve">Central Suriname Nature Reserve</a> seen from the Voltzberg</figcaption></figure>
<p>Most tourists visit Suriname for the <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity" title="Biodiversity">biodiversity</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Amazon_Basin" class="mw-redirect" title="Amazon Basin">Amazonian</a> rain forests in the south of the country, which are noted for their flora and fauna. The <a href="/wiki/Central_Suriname_Nature_Reserve" title="Central Suriname Nature Reserve">Central Suriname Nature Reserve</a> is the biggest and one of the most popular reserves, along with the <a href="/wiki/Brownsberg_Nature_Park" title="Brownsberg Nature Park">Brownsberg Nature Park</a> which overlooks the <a href="/wiki/Brokopondo_Reservoir" title="Brokopondo Reservoir">Brokopondo Reservoir</a>,  one of the largest human-made lakes in the world. In 2008, the <a href="/wiki/Berg_en_Dal_(Suriname)" class="mw-redirect" title="Berg en Dal (Suriname)">Berg en Dal Eco &amp; Cultural Resort</a> opened in Brokopondo.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Tonka_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Tonka Island (page does not exist)">Tonka Island</a> in the reservoir is home to a rustic <a href="/wiki/Eco-tourism" class="mw-redirect" title="Eco-tourism">eco-tourism</a> project run by the Saramaccaner Maroons.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pangi_(Maroon)" title="Pangi (Maroon)">Pangi</a> wraps and bowls made of calabashes are the two main products manufactured for tourists. The Maroons have learned that colorful and ornate pangis are popular with tourists.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> Other popular decorative souvenirs are hand-carved purple-hardwood made into bowls, plates, canes, wooden boxes, and wall decors.
</p><p>There are also many waterfalls throughout the country. Raleighvallen, or <a href="/w/index.php?title=Raleighfalls&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Raleighfalls (page does not exist)">Raleigh Falls</a>, is a 56,000-hectare (140,000-acre) nature reserve on the <a href="/wiki/Coppename_River" title="Coppename River">Coppename River</a>, rich in bird life. Also are the <a href="/wiki/Blanche_Marie_Falls" title="Blanche Marie Falls">Blanche Marie Falls</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Nickerie_River" title="Nickerie River">Nickerie River</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Wonotobo_Falls" title="Wonotobo Falls">Wonotobo Falls</a>. <a href="/wiki/Tafelberg,_Suriname" title="Tafelberg, Suriname">Tafelberg Mountain</a> in the centre of the country is surrounded by its own reserve – the <a href="/wiki/Tafelberg_Nature_Reserve" class="mw-redirect" title="Tafelberg Nature Reserve">Tafelberg Nature Reserve</a> – around the source of the <a href="/wiki/Saramacca_River" title="Saramacca River">Saramacca River</a>, as is the Voltzberg Nature Reserve further north on the Coppename River at Raleighvallen. In the interior are many <a href="/wiki/Maroon_(people)" class="mw-redirect" title="Maroon (people)">Maroon</a> and <a href="/wiki/Amerindian" class="mw-redirect" title="Amerindian">Amerindian</a> villages, many of which have their own reserves that are generally open to visitors.
</p><p>Suriname is one of the few countries in the world where at least one of each <a href="/wiki/Biome" title="Biome">biome</a> that the state possesses has been declared a wildlife reserve. Around 30% of the total land area of Suriname is protected by law as reserves.
</p><p>Other attractions include plantations such as <a href="/wiki/Laarwijk" title="Laarwijk">Laarwijk</a>, which is situated along the Suriname River. This plantation can be reached only by boat via Domburg, in the north central <a href="/wiki/Wanica_District" title="Wanica District">Wanica District</a> of Suriname.
</p><p>Crime rates continue to rise in Paramaribo and armed robberies are not uncommon. According to the current U.S. Department of State Travel Advisory at the date of the 2018 report's publication, Suriname has been assessed as Level 1: exercise normal precautions.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Landmarks">Landmarks</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Landmarks">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cathedral_Paramaribo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Cathedral_Paramaribo.jpg/170px-Cathedral_Paramaribo.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="239" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Cathedral_Paramaribo.jpg/255px-Cathedral_Paramaribo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Cathedral_Paramaribo.jpg/340px-Cathedral_Paramaribo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="390" data-file-height="549" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter_and_Paul_Cathedral,_Paramaribo" title="Saint Peter and Paul Cathedral, Paramaribo">Cathedral of St. Peter and Paul</a> in Paramaribo</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Jules_Wijdenbosch_Bridge" title="Jules Wijdenbosch Bridge">Jules Wijdenbosch Bridge</a> is a bridge over the river Suriname between Paramaribo and Meerzorg in the <a href="/wiki/Commewijne_District" title="Commewijne District">Commewijne district</a>. The bridge was built during the tenure of President <a href="/wiki/Jules_Albert_Wijdenbosch" class="mw-redirect" title="Jules Albert Wijdenbosch">Jules Albert Wijdenbosch</a> (1996–2000) and was completed in 2000. The bridge is 52 metres (171&#160;ft) high, and 1,504 metres (4,934&#160;ft) long. It connects Paramaribo with Commewijne, a connection which previously could only be made by ferry. The purpose of the bridge was to facilitate and promote the development of the eastern part of Suriname. The bridge consists of two lanes (one lane each way) and is not accessible to pedestrians.
</p><p>The construction of the <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter_and_Paul_Cathedral,_Paramaribo" title="Saint Peter and Paul Cathedral, Paramaribo">Sts. Peter and Paul Cathedral</a> started on 13 January 1883. Before it became a cathedral it was a theatre. The theatre was built in 1809 and burned down in 1820.
</p><p>Suriname is one of the few countries in the world where <a href="/wiki/Neveh_Shalom_Synagogue" title="Neveh Shalom Synagogue">a synagogue</a> is located next to <a href="/wiki/Mosque_Keizerstraat" title="Mosque Keizerstraat">a mosque</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
The two buildings are located next to each other in the centre of Paramaribo and have been known to share a parking facility during their respective religious rites, should they happen to coincide with one another.
</p><p>A relatively new landmark is the Hindu <a href="/wiki/Arya_Diwaker" title="Arya Diwaker">Arya Diwaker</a> temple in the Johan Adolf Pengelstraat in <a href="/wiki/Wanica" class="mw-redirect" title="Wanica">Wanica</a>, Paramaribo, which was inaugurated in 2001. A special characteristic of the temple is that it does not have images of the Hindu divinities, as they are forbidden in the <a href="/wiki/Arya_Samaj" title="Arya Samaj">Arya Samaj</a>, the Hindu movement to which the people who built the temple belong. Instead, the building is covered by many texts derived from the Vedas and other Hindu scriptures. The beautiful architecture makes the temple a tourist attraction.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Suriname&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-11">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Both <a href="/wiki/French_Guiana" title="French Guiana">French Guiana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands" title="Falkland Islands">Falkland Islands</a> are less extensive and populous, but they are an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_department" class="mw-redirect" title="Overseas department">overseas department</a> and <a href="/wiki/Overseas_region" class="mw-redirect" title="Overseas region">region</a> of France and an <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territory" class="mw-redirect" title="British Overseas Territory">overseas territory</a> of the United Kingdom respectively.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-15"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-15">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Suriname has been carbon negative since at least 2014.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-92"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-92">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The <a href="/wiki/International_Organization_for_Migration" title="International Organization for Migration">International Organization for Migration</a> made a confusion regarding the number of Surinamese migrants living in French Guiana. Their number is already included in the number for France (24,753 at the time of writing), as can be seen here: <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.insee.fr/fr/statistiques/3633212#tableau-Econtinent_radio1">données complémentaires</a>.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<ul><li><span style="font-size:85%;"><sup>1</sup> Member of the Community but not of the <a href="/wiki/CARICOM_Single_Market_and_Economy" title="CARICOM Single Market and Economy">CARICOM Single Market and Economy</a> (CSME)</span></li>
